Abstract: Expansion planning for extreme weather needs outage scenarios that specify which transmission branches fail. However, public outage records often only report customers without power by county, making it unclear how to exploit this information to create outage scenarios. We propose a method to recover sets of outaged branches that most closely match regional load shed data. This gives us sets of plausible outages for a given hazard event. We formulate a bilevel problem with a load shedding DC optimal power flow in the lower level. We relax the binary branch states to differentiate the lower level problem, replace it with its sensitivity matrix, and use gradient descent to update the upper level. We treat the relaxed branch states as probabilities and perform randomized rounding to restore integrality. On three test systems with synthetic outages, the recovered sets reduce the load shed error to $0.02-4.5\%$. On the California Test System with storm data from December 2025, the error falls to $23\%$. These scenarios give expansion planning a reliable source of outage data tied to observed hazards.

</details>

Abstract: Koopman operator theory represents nonlinear systems with linear and bilinear terms in the state and input observables, greatly simplifying control design. However, incorporating input constraints into Koopman-based controller synthesis remains challenging, limiting their applicability to constrained control tasks such as tracking. This paper develops a constrained Koopman Control Parametrization (cKoCoP) framework for synthesizing state-feedback controllers that satisfy prescribed input constraints with Lyapunov-certified stability of the identified lifted closed loop. The cKoCoP offers nonlinear state-feedback via linear-in-observables feedback, enabling linear controller design. The proposed controller is obtained through convex optimization: we incorporate ellipsoidal or polytopic input constraints with the S-procedure or Farkas-lemma-based conditions. We also introduce a Control Lyapunov Function (CLF) for trajectory tracking which incorporates input constraints. The approach is validated on two canonical nonlinear systems, a double pendulum and a unicycle, demonstrating data-driven tracking while respecting input constraints. Experiments validate the method's robustness to measurement noise, and runtime improvements over Koopman Model Predictive Control (KMPC) baselines. The results demonstrate that cKoCoP achieves competitive tracking performance with substantially reduced online computational cost, supporting its potential for real-time constrained tracking applications.

</details>

Abstract: Deploying voltage source converter (VSC)-based multi-terminal DC (MTDC) networks in existing AC grids offers a promising solution for alleviating transmission congestion, enhancing power-transfer capability, and improving operational economy. However, constructing an MTDC network requires participation from merchant investors. Therefore, the transmission system operator (TSO) responsible for the AC grid must provide an adequate economic incentive that enables merchant investors not only to recover the MTDC investment cost but also to earn a reasonable return commensurate with their risk and expectations. An insufficient incentive may fail to attract merchant investment, whereas an excessive incentive may unnecessarily reduce the economic benefits retained by the TSO. Determining an appropriate incentive that balances these competing interests therefore becomes a critical issue. To address this issue, this paper formulates a sharing-rate-parameterized mixed-integer second-order cone programming (MISOCP) model that jointly optimizes VSC siting and sizing while coordinating AC/DC system operation. A straightforward search procedure is then employed to identify the benefit-sharing rate that meets the investor's expectations while preserving sufficient benefits for the TSO. Case studies demonstrate the operating-cost benefits of MTDC integration and validate the proposed search strategy for seeking such a benefit-sharing rate.

</details>

Abstract: Evangelou, Stanković, Kevrekidis and Transtrum compared analytic and data-driven assessments of parameter identifiability for the infinite-bus synchronous generator. Fisher information with the manifold boundary approximation method (MBAM), and output diffusion maps with geometric harmonics, report the same six identifiable parameters on the late window $t \in [3, 5]\text{ s}$, and both place the inertia $H$ and the damping $D$ among the unidentifiable ones. We explain this result from the geometry of the dynamics rather than from that of the parameter-to-data map. The rotor swing is a fast fibre that has collapsed onto a two-dimensional normally attracting invariant manifold (NAIM) of the transient fluxes before the window opens. The zeroth-order NAIM is exactly independent of $H$, $D$, $T_{d0}''$ and $T_{q0}''$, four of the five MBAM limits. We prove, under stated smoothness and rank conditions, that each of the four limits is a boundary of the model manifold at a distance equal, to first order in the parameter, to its current profiled speed. The same analysis predicts how identifiability changes with the experiment: neither a larger initial speed deviation nor a load change restores $H$ or $D$ in the late window, whereas the early window $[0, 2]\text{ s}$ makes $H$ the best-determined parameter. Evaluated in a physical metric fixed by synchrophasor accuracy limits, the late window identifies five parameters rather than six and the early window nine. The spiked-covariance structure of the linearised trajectory map explains why the principal components of whitened, sampled trajectories reproduce the prior-scaled Fisher spectrum, and regression on those components attains the Fisher-predicted recovery error in 113 of 114 parameter--scenario pairs.

Abstract: Wireless power transfer (WPT) systems must maintain efficient and safe energy delivery under variations in geometry, coupling, load, alignment, and operating conditions. Machine-learning methods are increasingly applied to accelerate structural design, estimate operating parameters and states, and support adaptive control and scheduling. This review develops a task-oriented framework organized around three interconnected domains: structural design and optimization, parameter and state estimation, and control and optimization. Supervised learning, reinforcement learning, and hybrid or physics-informed methods are compared in terms of data requirements, dependence on physical models, information sources, computational requirements, accuracy, online response, application scope, and optimization objectives. Supervised learning provides rapid surrogate modeling, regression, and classification when representative labeled data are available. Reinforcement learning addresses sequential control and scheduling but introduces challenges in reward design, safe exploration, and simulation-to-reality transfer. Hybrid methods integrate physical knowledge with data-driven models to improve data efficiency and physical consistency. The principal barriers to engineering deployment include limited and biased datasets, weak out-of-distribution generalization, insufficient physical consistency, unsafe online adaptation, embedded-resource constraints, and the absence of standardized benchmarks. Future research should prioritize uncertainty-aware and physics-informed learning, safe simulation-to-reality transfer, lightweight edge deployment, distributed coordination, reproducible evaluation, and digital-twin-assisted development.

</details>

### [211] [A Matroidal Framework for Random Access in Linear Codes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28820)
*Daniella Bar-Lev,Hiram H. López,Gretchen L. Matthews,Flavio Salizzoni*

Main category: cs.IT

TL;DR: 线性码恢复平衡与对偶码同等；确定恢复平衡的通用定义；证明大多数稀疏铺路矩阵不满足。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 研究在随机采样线性码符号时的恢复效率，并探究恢复平衡性质与码对偶性的关系；同时扩展到任意矩阵的恢复平衡，了解稀疏铺路矩阵的性质。

Method: 通过将恢复期期数表达为相应矩阵的特征，推导与原码及其双的坐标级恒等式，利用该恒等式证明恢复平衡的双角色，并定义通用的恢复平衡概念，对稀疏铺路矩阵的极限行为进行分析。

Result: 1) 证明线性码恢复平衡⇔其对偶码恢复平衡，证实猜想。2) 定义任意矩阵的恢复平衡概念，3) 证明渐近几乎所有稀疏铺路矩阵不是恢复平衡的。

Conclusion: 确认线性码等价于其双对偶时恢复平衡，即线性码恢复平衡当且仅当其对偶码恢复平衡，证实了Gruica等人的猜想；并界定了任意矩阵的恢复平衡，并证明稀疏铺路矩阵的绝大多数在渐近上不满足恢复平衡。

Abstract: We study random access in linear codes when codeword symbols are sampled independently and uniformly with replacement. A code is recovery balanced if the expected number of samples required to recover a codeword coordinate is the same for every coordinate. We express the recovery expectations in terms of the associated matroid and derive a coordinatewise identity relating these values for a code and its dual. This proves that a linear code is recovery balanced if and only if its dual is recovery balanced, establishing a conjecture of Gruica et al. We further define the recovery balance for arbitrary matroids and show that asymptotically almost every sparse paving matroid is not recovery balanced.

</details>

Abstract: We prove state evolution for approximate message passing (AMP) with rectangular random matrices whose entries are independent, centered, and variance-normalized. The row and column dimensions grow with a ratio that converges to a positive finite limit. The entry laws may differ across coordinates and dimensions, and we assume uniform integrability of the fourth powers of the rescaled entries. For entries sampled independently from a fixed law, a finite fourth moment suffices, with no requirement for higher moments. The nonlinearities are continuously differentiable and globally Lipschitz, with bounded continuous first state derivatives. The signal and noise have independent and identically distributed coordinates with finite moments of all orders, and are independent of each other and of the matrix. At every fixed number of iterations, empirical averages of pseudo-Lipschitz functions of order two converge in probability to the limits specified by Gaussian state evolution. These averages include empirical second moments and products of trajectory coordinates. The proof combines an aggregate bound on third derivatives with randomized Lindeberg replacement and operator-norm truncation, without matching third moments or invoking a non-Gaussian universality theorem. We also show that independence of complementary projected components for every deterministic rank-one constraint pair characterizes Gaussian entries. This identifies the limitation of extending Gaussian conditioning identities to independent non-Gaussian matrices. Finally, a variance-profile example shows that average variance normalization alone does not ensure the homogeneous state evolution limits.

</details>


### [213] [From Common Information to Canonical Correlation Analysis and Beyond: Bridging Information Theory, Signal Processing, and Machine Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28978)
*Paris A. Karakasis,Walid Saad*

Main category: cs.IT

TL;DR: 整合CCA、信息论与工程方法，阐明多视角共通成分恢复的理论空间，并探讨非线性、自监督方向的可识别性与未来研究。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 在信号处理、机器学习和神经成像等领域，多组观测往往混合公共与私有信息，且常缺乏监督来分离公共成分。不同社区对此问题已分别研究，但缺乏统一视角。

Method: 以信息论为基底，先构建基本理论；随后分别介绍统计学的CCA及其多视角扩展、工程学的盲多用户检测与子空间方法，以及信息论的Gács–Körner与Wyner共通信息；再聚焦到非线性CCA（核、深度、概率模型），研究可识别性与自监督连接；最后讨论开放问题。

Result: 展示了各方法的相互对应关系，明确了何种情形下可以实现精确等价，提出了非线性框架下可识别性条件，并与自监督学习建立了联系，为后续研究提供了系统框架与启发。

Conclusion: 本文阐明并统一了统计学、信息论与工程领域对多视角共通成分恢复的主要理论与方法，指出它们之间的等价性与区别，并讨论了在非线性与自监督学习框架下的可识别性条件与未来扩展方向。

Abstract: Many problems in signal processing, machine learning, and neuroimaging provide several jointly observed measurements of one process, each mixing a component common to all of them with a component private to itself, and require the common component to be recovered without supervision. This problem has been studied in several communities, largely independently. Statisticians proposed canonical correlation analysis (CCA), introduced by Hotelling in 1936. Information theorists proposed two notions of common information, due to Gács and Körner and to Wyner. Engineers developed subspace methods for sensor arrays and multiuser communications to design multiview receivers. This tutorial presents the main results and tools each community developed for this problem in a single exposition, and explains the connections that emerge where they meet, distinguishing exact equivalences from special cases and analogies. We develop the information-theoretic foundation from first principles. We then introduce CCA and its extension to several views, followed by the engineering thread from blind multiuser detection to CCA as a blind receiver. Next, we turn to nonlinear CCA, covering kernel, deep, and probabilistic formulations, the conditions under which the common factor is identifiable, and connections to self-supervised learning. We conclude with a discussion of further extensions and open directions. The exposition is self-contained, so that readers from each of these communities can follow the parts developed by the others.

</details>

*Tue Do,Daniel Alabi*

Main category: cs.IT

TL;DR: 提供精确敏感度与噪声校准框架，显著提升扩散模型的隐私‑实用性，支持多种隐私标准，并通过半正定规划实现误差最小化。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 传统方法在扩散模型中使用松散的敏感度界和各向同性高斯噪声，导致不必要的误差。需要一种能精确界定敏感度、优化噪声量并保障多种隐私形态的框架。

Method: 1) 用两个记录推导最坏情况敏感度的精确表述；2) 在仅需最多 \(m(m-1)\) 个约束的条件下计算敏感度；3) 对 Rényi、近似、Gaussian 与二进制记录互信息隐私给出精确安全分析；4) 将加权均方误差最小化转化为半正定规划求解，并证明其对偶问题的精确对齐。

Result: - 精确敏感度只需考虑两条记录，约束数不随样本量增长；- 传统直径界可被显著收紧，噪声校准可获得无界改进；- 对多种隐私形式给出不比先前转换更松的精确保证；- 用半正定规划求解加权均方误差最小化，强对偶性保证全局最优性。

Conclusion: 本文提出一种最优的隐私‑实用平衡框架，精确描述扩散模型中的敏感度并给出最优噪声校准方法，显著改进了传统的过度保守估计，并为多种隐私衡量提供了精确保证；同时，通过半正定规划实现了误差最小化并证明了强对偶性。

Abstract: Existing approaches to differential privacy in diffusion models rely on loose sensitivity bounds and isotropic Gaussian noise, leading to unnecessary estimation error. We develop a framework for the optimal privacy-utility tradeoff of ideal diffusion denoisers, replacing conservative bounds with exact sensitivity characterizations and provably optimal noise calibration. First, we show that worst-case sensitivity over datasets of arbitrary size can be characterized exactly using only two records. For a data domain of size $m$, this reduces the analysis to at most $m(m-1)$ constraints, regardless of dataset size. Our characterization can be arbitrarily tighter than conventional diameter-based sensitivity bounds, revealing potentially unbounded improvements in noise calibration. Second, we obtain exact privacy guarantees for Rényi, approximate, and Gaussian differential privacy, as well as mutual-information privacy for binary records, avoiding the looseness of previous generic privacy conversions. Finally, we show that the minimum achievable weighted mean-squared error can be computed through a semidefinite program and prove strong duality, providing certificates of global optimality.

</details>

Abstract: We characterize the minimax sample complexity of estimating Rényi entropy from independent samples on an alphabet of size at most $k$, when the known order $α>1$ may vary with $k$. For every fixed additive accuracy $\varepsilon\in(0,1/2)$ and success probability $2/3$, we prove matching bounds with constants independent of $k$ and $α$. For $1<α<\log k$, the rate is $Θ_\varepsilon(αk^{1-1/α}X_α(k,\varepsilon))$, where a monotone scalar inequality defines $X_α$ at noninteger orders. The bounds remain uniform as the order approaches one or an integer, or grows with the alphabet. Integer orders below $\log k$ have rate $Θ_\varepsilon(αk^{1-1/α})$; orders at least $\log k$ and min-entropy have rate $Θ_\varepsilon(k\log k)$.
  We extend the analysis to logarithms of positive additive functionals. For every fixed continuous $f$ satisfying $f(0)=0$, $f(x)>0$ for $x>0$, and $f(x)\sim ax^r$ at zero, with $a>0$ and $r>1$, estimating $-β^{-1}\log\sum_i f(p_i)$ at fixed accuracy, for fixed $β>0$, has sample complexity $Θ(k^{1-1/r})$ for integer $r$ and $Θ(k/\log k)$ otherwise. A replication reduction preserves the sampling experiment and transfers the rates for powers. Smooth flat functions show that regularity alone does not determine the complexity.
  The upper bound combines polynomial approximation, Gamma count statistics, and an independent pilot, with a global affine correction near order one. The lower bound uses interpolation to construct positive mean-one priors and controls their distinguishability and entropy separation after normalization. Exact count-variance identities and weighted approximation duality connect the two arguments.

</details>

Abstract: In this work we formalize a new class of binary trees called encoding trees. We extend this structure in two ways, through labelling to create the class of labelled encoding trees, and pruning to create the class of pruned encoding trees. We discuss how these two extensions can be combined to create the the class of pruned labelled encoding trees. Using the supplementary results that we prove about these classes of trees, we construct a code that uses pruned labelled encoding trees to convert a string of symbols from a sequence of non-empty sets to a binary string. We prove that this code is a variable-length prefix code that can process any valid string of symbols from the given sequence of non-empty sets. Furthermore, we construct a code from the class of pruned labelled encoding trees that goes in the opposite direction, from binary strings to a string of symbols from a sequence of non-empty sets of arbitrary symbols. We prove that this code is also a variable-length prefix code that can process any binary string of any length, provided that the sequence of non-empty sets is sufficiently long. We provide an equation that establishes the minimum sequence length to encode any binary string of a given length, based on that length.

</details>

Abstract: MDS array codes achieve optimal storage efficiency in distributed storage systems, but their repair bandwidth depends critically on the sub-packetization level. While the theoretically optimal repair bandwidth (i.e., cut-set bound) of a high-rate exact-repair MDS array code is attainable with sufficiently large sub-packetization, the fundamental limits for fixed sub-packetization remain poorly understood. We study this problem for centralized linear exact repair of $h$ failures from $d$ helper nodes over a fixed finite field.
  We show that the rank profile of any linear repair scheme is constrained by two auxiliary mixed-alphabet codes. The first code is formed from the kernels of the helper projections and exists for all repair degrees; the second is formed from their row spaces and applies in the full-helper regime when $d\ge r$. These codes have complementary alphabet profiles, and their size and minimum-distance requirements are forced solely by the MDS property and repair correctness. This gives a unified route from classical coding-theoretic upper bounds to repair-bandwidth lower bounds. As a consequence, we obtain several explicit lower bounds via Singleton, Hamming, Plotkin, and Griesmer arguments, including known bounds as corollaries. We further formulate a Delsarte-type support-enumerator LP for mixed-alphabet codes and give an exact type-compressed implementation. For fixed sub-packetization \(\ell\), this yields a polynomial-time procedure for evaluating the resulting finite-parameter lower bound.

</details>

For a cyclic Frobenius-orbit polynomial, the multiple equal-difference structure determines its support period, core degree, and least relative degree of an unramified extension over which all its basic-irreducible factors are binomials.
  Coordinate projections give every constacyclic code over a finite commutative ring a unique maximal interleaving into copies of one support-primitive code. At simple-root lengths over \(A\), the intrinsic period of a code is the greatest common divisor of the periods of its torsion codes. The decomposition yields distance and duality formulas and the arithmetic Singleton bound for free codes. At every length, a nonzero code over \(A\) has the same minimum Hamming distance as its highest torsion code, which gives a refined bound. At repeated-root lengths, identical torsion filtrations can occur with different intrinsic periods, while a class of nonzero codes obtained by substitution from simple-root generators retains the same core and minimum distance.
  Trace matrices recover the coefficients of basic-irreducible cores using unit pivots, and local Hensel corrections lift compressed factors directly. For \(X^n-1\) with \(p\nmid n\), lifting and output have quasi-linear bit cost in the compressed representation when the arithmetic data and residue factorizations are given.

</details>

Abstract: In this paper, we develop a dual-function waveform design framework for multiple-input multiple-output integrated sensing and communication (MIMO-ISAC) systems operating in congested electromagnetic environments. In such environments, the dual-function waveform must coexist with legacy systems, such as localized positioning sensors, maritime radar networks, and satellite communication links, that occupy specific frequency bands and angular sectors. To enable precision coexistence, we incorporate localized angular-spectral region constraints that suppress beamformed energy only within the angle-frequency regions occupied by each legacy system, while preserving the spatial degrees of freedom (DoFs) available outside those regions. The resulting waveform design problem is formulated as a nonconvex optimization problem and solved using a low-complexity majorization--minimization alternating direction method of multipliers (MM--ADMM) algorithm. Simulation results demonstrate that the proposed framework provides precise legacy-system protection while maintaining robust radar sensing and multi-user communication performance.

Abstract: For every prime power $q>19683$, we settle the Cheng--Murray deep-hole problem for full-length Reed--Solomon codes in every dimension $2\le k\le q-2$, with the characteristic-two cases stated explicitly. For odd $q$, the deep holes are precisely the words of degree $k$. For even $q$ and $k\ge3$, the same conclusion holds except for an additional family when $k=q-3$. In even characteristic and dimension two, every deep hole has a unique expression $a h(X)+bX+c$, where $a\ne0$ and $h$ is a normalized $o$-polynomial. The proof combines quadratic quotient algebras, graph-theoretic weighted independent-set inequalities, and planar arc completion. We also obtain normal rational curve rigidity in the corresponding nonexceptional dimensions, the projective Reed--Solomon covering radii, and distinct-coordinate solvability and remainder criteria.

Abstract: Pinching antenna systems (PASS) provide flexible transmitter-side radiation reconfiguration for high-frequency wireless communications. This paper applies PASS to non-orthogonal multiple access (NOMA) covert communication networks, where activated pinching antennas (PAs) transmit superimposed signals to a pair of users stochastically distributed within a rectangle. Two passive wardens respectively monitor the downlink transmissions intended for two users. More specifically, we derive the closed-form detection error probability (DEP) expressions for two wardens under bounded noise uncertainty. Then we analytically determine the optimal detection thresholds and minimum DEPs in the worst-case covert detection scenarios. The covert transmission rates of two users are further characterized by taking into consideration ideal successive interference cancellation and non-ideal successive interference cancellation at near user and intra-NOMA interference at far user. Numerical results are presented to confirm that: 1) PASS-NOMA is capable of providing higher covert rates relative to conventional orthogonal multiple access OMA schemes; 2) As the number of activated PAs begun to increase, the near user has ability to obtain a larger covert rate gain than far user; and 3) The DEP of PASS-NOMA networks is larger than that of both PASS-OMA and traditional antenna (TA) based NOMA in specific output power regions.

</details>

Abstract: We propose a joint source-channel coding (JSCC) framework for efficient wireless point cloud transmission. In the proposed scheme, the point cloud is first divided into local patches and transformed into compact latent representations, which are then mapped to symbols for transmission over a noisy channel. Our model incorporates an entropy model that exploits inter-patch spatial dependencies to estimate patch-level coding costs and allocate different numbers of symbols accordingly. Unlike existing patch-based point cloud JSCC methods that use the same transmission rate between patches, we adapt the channel usage of each patch latent representation. To exploit inter-patch dependencies without patch-by-patch autoregressive processing, we select a group of anchor patches whose features provide contextual information for entropy estimation of non-anchor ones. The framework is trained using a reconstruction loss and an entropy-based rate surrogate. Experiments on ModelNet40 demonstrate overall rate-distortion improvements over existing baselines across channel conditions.

</details>

Abstract: Beamforming is a key enabling technology for centimeter-wave Gigantic MIMO (G-MIMO) systems, using antenna arrays to generate wide beams for cell-wide broadcasting or narrow beams for data transmission. This paper presents a novel \emph{beam shaping technology} for designing environment-aware broadcast beams (i.e., beams for common control information delivery, as in 5G SSB). As a case study, we shape a \emph{prostrate beam} in the vertical plane to approximate uniform electromagnetic power distribution across the ground, thereby enhancing broadcast coverage quality. We first propose a geometric broadcasting model that, through coordinate transformation, relates base station height, beam spread, maximum service distance, and downtilt angle. Building on this model and the signal propagation model, we formulate the beam shaping problem as a non-convex optimization problem that aims to maximize ground-level coverage. To solve it, we develop a generalized optimization framework. We reformulate the problem into a matrix representation, equivalently transforming it into a target pursuit optimization (TPO) problem. Using variable substitution, the objective is convexified into a quadratically constrained quadratic program, and the non-convex rank constraint is handled via successive convex approximation, decomposing the problem into iteratively solved subproblems. We provide complexity and convergence analyses for the proposed algorithm. Numerical simulations confirm that the optimization effectively shapes the intended beam pattern and that the proposed prostrate beam yields significant performance gains, including substantially extended coverage distance, over conventional wide-beam benchmarks.

</details>

TL;DR: ComRSI通过LLM辅助，递归优化通信程序，显著提升语义通信的准确率与速率。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 传统语义通信编程需先预设程序，无法动态适配通道与计算资源；本文旨在通过递归自我改进实现程序的自适应优化。

Method: 该框架ComRSI利用大型语言模型辅助代码编辑，通过固定代理逐步修改编码器、通道适配器、解码器、损失函数及训练策略，受限评估器根据预算及资源约束筛选候选方案，接受的代码和文本反馈用于后续迭代。对十代CIFAR-100实验，八次接受修订后得到注意力池化代码，显著提升速率与准确率。

Result: 在AWGN通道下，使用32个复符号即可达74.3%准确率，显著优于初始设计的256符号状态；在衰落通道及Tiny-ImageNet上也表现一致。重复实验显示，采用递归方案的平均最终得分高于无递归或只使用文本历史的代理。

Conclusion: 递归式程序优化在语义通信中的有效性得到验证，提升了准确率与资源利用率，尽管仍存在速率-鲁棒性和计算资源分配的权衡待进一步探索。

Abstract: Semantic communication jointly optimizes inference accuracy, channel use, and computation, yet the underlying communication program is typically specified before training. This paper presents ComRSI (Communications-Oriented Recursive Self-Improvement), a framework for recursively evolving that program through large-language-model-assisted code edits. A fixed agent modifies the encoder, channel adapters, decoder, loss, and training policy; a protected evaluator screens candidates under prescribed budgets and resource constraints. Accepted code and textual feedback inform subsequent proposals, while candidate weights are trained from scratch. In the original ten-generation CIFAR-100 lineage, eight accepted revisions produce an attention-pooled code achieving 74.3% test accuracy over an Additive White Gaussian Noise (AWGN) channel at 32 complex symbols per image, compared with 67.2% at 256 symbols for the initial design. The program also improves fading-channel accuracy and transfers to Tiny-ImageNet after retraining. A repeat study with ten searches per method compares ComRSI against non-recursive agents with and without textual history. Mean final search scores balancing accuracy, transmission rate, and computational cost are 0.7587, 0.7376, and 0.7089, respectively; the mean numbers of accepted revisions per search are 6.0, 5.0, and 2.1. The advantage over the memoryless agent is supported by a two-sided rank test, whereas the difference from the history-equipped agent remains statistically inconclusive at this sample size. Archived proposals show that pooling can yield a large improvement, while retained history supports subsequent refinement. These results support recursive program optimization within the evaluated setting and expose its remaining rate-robustness and compute-placement trade-offs.

</details>

Abstract: Pinching-antenna systems (PASS) flexibly reconfigure the wireless channel by positioning dielectric antennas along a waveguide near each user. Releasing and re-attaching an antenna, however, is a mechanical operation orders of magnitude slower than electronic switching, wasting much transmission time. Existing designs generally assume instant reconfiguration, neglect the mechanical latency, or keep the antenna fixed. This paper proposes S-PASS, a star architecture of waveguides sharing one radio-frequency chain through a switch, and pipelined antenna pre-positioning (PAP), a time-division protocol in which idle waveguides pre-position their antennas for their next users while one transmits. It derives in closed form the scheduling efficiency, the number of waveguides beyond which the latency costs no transmission time, and the latency up to which PAP outperforms fixed placement. Analytical and numerical results show that this latency grows with each added waveguide, and users at the outer ends of the waveguides gain most.

</details>

Abstract: Let $\mathcal{C}$ be an almost maximum distance separable (AMDS) code over the finite fields with parameters $[n,k,n-k]$ whose minimum-weight codewords support an $(n-k-1)$-$(n,n-k,λ)$ design. We first determine the parameters of the dual code $\mathcal{C}^{\bot}$. We also characterize the design and its complementary design supported by the minimum weight codewords of $\mathcal{C}^{\bot}$. In particular, we present explicit formulas for the weight enumerators of all subset codes of $\mathcal{C}^{\bot}$ with length at least $k+1$. These formulas are completely determined by the cardinality of the subset, the dimension, together with the parameter $λ$, and are independent of the specific choice of subsets. As an application, we answer an open problem posed by Wang, Tang and Ding in 2023 by proving that the $3$-design supported by the minimum-weight codewords in their proposed cyclic dual code is isomorphic to the complementary design of the Witt spherical geometry design.

Abstract: We study the set of weights occurring in the high-order binary Reed-Muller codes $RM(m-7,m)$. For every $m\geq 14$, we prove that the weight spectrum contains all even weights in $[336,2^m-336]$, together with an explicit finite set of low weights and their complements. The proof first establishes the corresponding base-code inclusion in $RM(7,14)$. This base step uses four-block concatenation, explicit degree-six monomial witnesses, and known spectrum inputs for $RM(5,10)$ and $RM(6,12)$. The base inclusion is then lifted to all $m\geq 14$ by a concatenation-based induction. We also analyze the remaining base-code target weights $322,326,330,334$ in several restricted families, including four-block branches, affine-flat and structured inner corrections in a fixed Carlet-type Maiorana-McFarland outer shell, and sparse sums of degree-7 coordinate monomials. The residual analysis delineates several construction routes that cannot yield the four unresolved base-code weights, thereby sharpening the remaining $c=7$ spectrum problem.

Abstract: We study the maximum length of nontrivial simple-root cyclic maximum distance separable (MDS) codes over ${\mathbb F}_q$. The proofs combine Fourier supports, locality bounds and field descent with arithmetic restrictions on multiplicative orders and prime-adic valuations.

Abstract: In secure summation, $K$ parties each hold an input, and the sum of the inputs is computed at one party, securely against at most $T$ colluding parties. Communication typically takes one hop: each party sends one message directly to the party that computes the sum, using correlated randomness that is independent of the inputs and held in advance. We consider interactive secure summation, where every pair of parties is connected by a secure link, so that each party can send messages to every other party, each party has only independent local randomness, and the messages may depend on the inputs and on earlier messages over multiple rounds. We characterize the capacity region, the set of achievable rates of communication, counting all messages exchanged among the parties over all rounds, and of the randomness of every party, counted in symbols per symbol of the sum. For $0\le T\le K-2$, the optimal communication rate is $K(K-1)/(K-T)$, and the randomness rates form a polyhedron with, up to a relabeling of the parties, a single extreme point: $T$ parties have the randomness rates $(K+T-2t)/(K-T)$, $t=1,\ldots,T$, and the others none. In particular, the optimal total randomness rate is $T(K-1)/(K-T)$. Linear schemes achieve every extreme point together with the optimal communication rate. The same techniques give the capacity region when every party recovers the sum and when the sum is computed at a server without input.

</details>

Abstract: We consider the problem of binary sequence reconstruction where a sequence is transmitted multiple times over a channel, and each undergoes substitution errors by an arbitrary error vector of Hamming weight exactly $t$. We determine $N_{\mathrm{wc}}$, the minimum number of distinct channel output observations guaranteeing unique reconstruction by an optimal decoder, and show that the bit-wise majority-vote decoder, which was proved by Levenshtein (2001) to be optimal for channels corrupting up to $t$ bits, is in fact suboptimal here. Despite this suboptimality, we prove a condition that admits a practical early-termination rule, necessary for every decoder at any number of outputs $N$, and sufficient for bit-wise majority-vote even below $N_{\mathrm{wc}}$. We evaluate the number of outputs at which this condition is triggered, numerically, and show that it frequently occurs below $N_{\mathrm{wc}}$ when the block-length $k>2t+1$. Finally, we propose an alternative algorithm aimed at guaranteeing unique reconstruction with fewer observations than bit-wise majority-vote, and demonstrate through exhaustive enumeration that it successfully decodes channel output-sets where bit-wise majority-vote fails.
